金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 27 日
上週重點訊息
◼ AI 投資回報疑慮升溫，資金轉向債市避險：市場對大型科技企業 AI 基礎建設投資規模、融資
需求及未來獲利回收能力的疑慮升溫，導致科技股賣壓擴大，10 月 22 日 Nasdaq 指數下跌
約 2.6%。 隨著市場風險偏好下降 ， 避險資金轉向美國公債 ，10 年期公債殖利率單日下降約 19
個基點，創 2023 年 3 月以來最大單日跌幅，並於 23 日回升至 4.99%。市場關注焦點逐漸由
AI 投資規模轉向資本支出效益，後續大型科技企業財報及現金流表現，將成為評估 AI 產業評

價合理性的重要依據。
◼ 中東衝突推升通膨風險：中東衝突持續升溫，伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝攻擊沙烏地阿拉伯，引
發市場對原油供應中斷的擔憂，推升布蘭特原油價格於10 月 23 日升至每桶 116.72 美元，單
週上漲約 6.2%，創 2022 年以來最高週收盤水準。能源價格攀升加劇全球通膨壓力，並壓抑
市場風險偏好。
◼ 法國政治動盪加劇財政疑慮，歐洲主權債風險重新受到關注：法國因生活成本上升爆發大規模
抗議，進一步加深市場對政府財政整頓能力的疑慮。10 月 19 日法國 10 年期公債殖利率上升
約 10 個基點至 4.88%附近，與德國公債利差擴大至約 144 個基點，創 2012 年以來最高收盤
水準 ， 法股單日下跌約1.5%， 歐元同步走弱 。 市場擔憂政治不確定性可能削弱財政改革進度 ，
並透過主權債評價影響銀行業資產負債表。後續須關注法國財政政策、歐洲主權債利差及銀行
融資成本變化。
市場展望
◼ 科技股短線宜審慎，中期聚焦基本面穩健的 AI 企業：近期 AI 資本支出、融資需求及評價調整

使科技股波動加劇 ， 但先進製程與半導體設備需求仍具韌性 ，AI 中長期成長趨勢未改變 。 隨市
場焦點由成長題材轉向投資效益，企業營收與訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率的重要性提
升 ， 具技術優勢 、 財務穩健及獲利能見度的企業相對有利 ， 而高度依賴外部融資者宜審慎看待 。
◼ 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：市場風險偏好降溫時，美國公債仍具一定
避險效果。儘管 Fed 貨幣政策路徑與能源價格增添利率不確定性，短天期債券易受政策利率
牽動，超長天期債券面臨財政赤字、國債供給及期限溢價等壓力，相較之下，中天期美債的風
險報酬較為均衡。信用債方面，投資等級企業基本面及現金流仍具韌性，後續仍可持續關注。

2308 台達電(Delta)

報告日期： 2026/11/02 研究員：曾智楷 (Kevin Tseng)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 營收 1970.6 億 ， 毛利率34.1%， 營利率16.6%，EPS 9.04
元。受到供應鏈缺料以及拉貨遞延影響， 3Q26 毛利率不如預
期。  
 預估 4Q26 營收將維持成長趨勢，毛利率部分，隨著隨著庫存
預計開始去化，以及部分產品漲價部分產品為反映原物料成本
而調漲價格，毛利率將有望回升。  
 HVDC 部分， ±400V 已在下半年開始出貨， 800V 系統亦持續
發展推進，同時液冷、電源機櫃、 DC-DC 模組等新產品線持續
放量；公司亦持續推進固態氧化物燃料電池 (SOFC) ，目前已有
海外大型客戶接觸送樣，初期產能逾 100MW ，預計 2027 年正
式量產。

一、受缺料影響， 3Q26 毛利率 34.1% ，EPS 9.04 元 
台達電 3Q26 營收 1970.6 億， QoQ +7.5% ，YoY +31.1% ，略低
於公司預期，主要由於供應鏈缺料以及資料中心建置延遲，導致
部分貨品出貨遞延 。 就產品組合來看 ，3Q26 電源及零部件 (Power 
Electronics) 佔整體營收的 54% ，交通 (Mobility) 佔 5% ，自動化
(Automation) 佔 8% 基礎建設 (Infrastructure) 佔 33% 。而獲利方
面， 3Q26 營業毛利 672 億， QoQ +2.8% ，YoY +28.1% ，毛利率
34.1% ，QoQ -1.6ppts ，YoY -0.8ppts ，公司表示，第三季存貨增
加，認列約 39 億元的備抵存貨跌價損失。主要由於現有產品缺
料，以及新產品客戶新平台量產較慢、拉貨延後所致，預計第四
季至明年第一季陸續消化並回沖。 3Q26 營業淨利 326.2 億，營利
率 16.6% ，業外淨利 10.46 億，稅後淨利 234.95 億，單季稅後
EPS 9.04 元。

二、庫存去化、部分產品漲價， 4Q26 營運有望回升  
展望 4Q26 ，公司表示，第四季營收確定將維持成長趨勢，而毛利
率部分，公司亦預估，第四季毛利率將優於第三季，主要由於隨
著庫存預計從第四季開始去化，並在明年第一季陸續消化完畢，
而存貨順利出貨後即可回沖。另外，第四季部分產品為反映原物
料成本而調漲價格 。 預估4Q26 營收 2181 億，QoQ +10.7% ，YoY 
+35.0% ，毛利率 35.5% 。營利率 18.2% ，稅後淨利 283 億，單季
稅後 EPS 10.89 元。

投資評等：買進 
目標價位：1680 元 
     27x-2027 EPS 
參考價位：1335 元 
     21.3 X 2027 EPS 
     2026/10/30 收盤

三. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 37.53/62.49 元 
展望 2026 年，公司表示， 2026 年全年整體資料中心相關營收佔
比將接近 5 成，其中， AI 電源解決方案營收佔比有望達 25% ，液
冷散熱則大約佔 13% 。預估 2026 全年營收 7574 億元， YoY 
+36.5% ，毛利率 35.5% ，營利率 17.3% ，稅後淨利 974.8 億，全
年稅後 EPS 37.53 元。展望 2027 年，受惠於 CSP 持續擴大資本
支出， AI 資料中心建置需求延續，公司預期， AI 相關營收與液冷
系統產品 2027 年的營收佔比將進一步攀升。 HVDC 部分， ±400V
已在下半年開始出貨 ， 由NVIDIA 推動的 800V 系統亦持續發展推
進，同時液冷、電源機櫃、 DC-DC 模組等新產品線持續放量，此
外，公司亦持續推進固態變壓器 (SST) 與固態氧化物燃料電池
(SOFC) ，初期產能逾 100MW ，預計 2027 年正式量產，目前已有
海外大型客戶接觸送樣。而產能部分，公司亦宣布，將於馬來西
亞檳城設立大型生產據點，預計五年內產能追上泰國廠區。另外，
針對 Vicor 提出侵權訴訟以及美國 ITC 啟動調查，公司則表示，
公司自身擁有多項 IP，且 ITC 程序冗長，短期內對公司出貨與客
戶不會造成影響。預估 2027 全年營收 1 兆 695 億元， YoY 
+41.2% ，毛利率 36.5% ，營利率 20.0% ，稅後淨利 1623 億，全
年稅後 EPS 62.49 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 1680 元 
考量到 CSP 持續擴大資本支出， AI 資料中心建置需求延續，公司
具備關鍵產業地位，且漲價效益及遞延訂單後續將陸續反映，將
有望持續挹注公司營收及獲利成長動能。以公司 2027 年 EPS 來
看，目前股價交易於 2027 年 EPS 約 21~22 倍本益比，評價仍具
上修空間，給於買進投資建議，目標價 1680 元(27x 2027 EPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
台達電 (Delta) 成立於 1971 年，以製造繞線式磁性元件起家，早期
專注於筆電 (NB) 、桌機 (DT) 、螢幕 (Monitor) 、伺服器 (Server) 、印
表機 (Printer) 及網通等 IT 相關產品之電源供應器與散熱風扇，憑
藉深厚的核心技術延伸發展，穩居全球磁性與散熱元件重要供應
商及全球最大交換式電源供應器龍頭。因應全球電子產業轉變與
環保節能意識抬頭，台達電近年積極跨足工業自動化、智慧安防
及綠能領域，逐步從關鍵零組件製造商轉型為「整體節能解決方
案提供者」，成為全球電源管理與散熱解決方案的專業領導廠商。
目前集團深耕電源及零組件、自動化與基礎設施等核心業務，並
進一步拓展至交通領域，依 2025 年產品結構比重來看，分別為電

圖一、台達電產品營收佔比

資料來源：台達電

二.產業趨勢概況  
1. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP 、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google 、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型 (LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28% 更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、 GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中， Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展， 2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。 AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。 Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR 、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

2. 液冷散熱成高階 AI 基礎設施標配，估 2026 年滲透率達 53% 
根據 TrendForce 最新研究顯示，隨著 AI 基礎設施建置浪潮及高
階晶片運算功率不斷攀升，單晶片熱設計功耗已超越 1kW ，使液

冷散熱技術正式成為高階 AI 基礎設施的標準配置。在 NVIDIA 、
AMD 與 Google 等巨頭的高階晶片與整櫃式方案強力推動下，預
估 2026 年液冷散熱於 AI 晶片的滲透率將從 2025 年的 33% 大幅
躍升至 53%，並在 2027 年進一步逼近 60%。其中， Google 因積
極導入自研 TPU 並率先全面推廣整櫃式液冷設計，成為主要推手
之一。此外，隨著 NVIDIA Vera Rubin 平台全面導入全液冷架構，
水冷板、分歧管及冷卻分配系統等關鍵零組件需求隨之大增，帶
動相關供應鏈如 AVC、Cooler Master 等業者積極搶攻各大 CSP
與 AI 平台商機。

圖三、 2023-2027 年液冷散熱於 AI 晶片滲透率預估

資料來源： TrendForce 、宏遠投顧整理

3. 預估 2026 年全球資料中心用電需求年增 31% 
根據  TrendForce 產業研究顯示 ， 受惠於AI 應用需求激增與雲端
服務供應商 (CSP) 加速擴建基礎設施 ， 全球資料中心電力需求容量
預計將由 2025 年的 122.9GW 攀升至 2026 年的 161GW ，YoY 
+31% ，並於 2027 年維持超過 30% 的高成長率；其中， AI 伺服器
為核心增長動能，占整體電力需求比重由 2025 年的 25% 提升至
2026 年的 33.4% ，預計 2027 年更將突破 40% ，同步帶動散熱與
供電等非 IT 設備需求，促使資料中心規劃導入高壓直流供電
(HVDC) 以優化供配電效率。然而，電網供電增速卻未能跟上需求
擴張 ， 全球資料中心電力供需自2026 年起開始背離 ， 並將於2028
年後因電網併網與輸配電建設交付遞延而顯著擴大；預估至 2030
年，全球資料中心電力需求將達 490.7GW ，但電網可供容量僅
222.6GW ，供需缺口高達 268GW( 其中資料中心部署量最大的美
國自 2028 年起遞延加劇，至 2030 年缺口預計超過 170GW) ，未
來此一實質短缺與帳面落差能否透過業者自建發電及電網改善來
填補，仍有待觀察。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 庫存去化、部分產品漲價， 4Q26 營運有望回升  
展望 4Q26 ，公司表示，第四季營收確定將維持成長趨勢，而毛利
率部分，公司亦預估，第四季毛利率將優於第三季，主要由於隨
著庫存預計從第四季開始去化，並在明年第一季陸續消化完畢，
而存貨順利出貨後即可回沖。另外，第四季部分產品為反映原物
料成本而調漲價格 。 預估4Q26 營收 2181 億，QoQ +10.7% ，YoY 
+35.0% ，毛利率 35.5% 。營利率 18.2% ，稅後淨利 283 億，單季
稅後 EPS 10.89 元。

二. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 37.53/62.49 元 
展望 2026 年，公司表示， 2026 年全年整體資料中心相關營收佔
比將接近 5 成，其中， AI 電源解決方案營收佔比有望達 25% ，液
冷散熱則大約佔 13% 。預估 2026 全年營收 7574 億元， YoY 
+36.5% ，毛利率 35.5% ，營利率 17.3% ，稅後淨利 974.8 億，全
年稅後 EPS 37.53 元。展望 2027 年，受惠於 CSP 持續擴大資本
支出， AI 資料中心建置需求延續，公司預期， AI 相關營收與液冷
系統產品 2027 年的營收佔比將進一步攀升。 HVDC 部分， ±400V
已在下半年開始出貨 ， 由NVIDIA 推動的 800V 系統亦持續發展推
進，同時液冷、電源機櫃、 DC-DC 模組等新產品線持續放量，此
外，公司亦持續推進固態變壓器 (SST) 與固態氧化物燃料電池
(SOFC) ，初期產能逾 100MW ，預計 2027 年正式量產，目前已有
海外大型客戶接觸送樣。而產能部分，公司亦宣布，將於馬來西
亞檳城設立大型生產據點，預計五年內產能追上泰國廠區。另外，
針對 Vicor 提出侵權訴訟以及美國 ITC 啟動調查，公司則表示，
公司自身擁有多項 IP，且 ITC 程序冗長，短期內對公司出貨與客

股價相對大盤走勢

聯發科 (2454 TT)  Trading Buy

聯發科的第二個 ASIC 客戶與專案將於 1Q28 貢獻營收，投
資建議給 Trading Buy 。  
投資建議 ： 4Q26 、 2026 全年展望優於先前預期，第二個 ASIC
客戶與專案將於 1Q28 貢獻營收，投資建議給 Trading Buy 。  
聯發科 3Q26 獲利季增、年增： 聯發科 3Q26 營收 1,680.58 億
元， QoQ+10.43%\YoY+18.27% ；稅後純益 278.82 億元，
QoQ+14.58%\YoY+10.80% ；稅後 EPS 17.37 元。  
4Q26 、 2026 全年展望優於預期： 財務指引：假設匯率為 31.7 ，
聯發科估 4Q26 營收， 2,101~2,218 億台幣， QoQ+25~32% ，毛利
率 45±1.5% 、費用率 26±2% 。 2026 全年營收將優於先前預期的
美元營收高個位數成長，主因為 AI 加速器業務放量與漲價。  
AI ASIC 業務的營益率高於公司平均： AI ASIC 業務的毛利率
低於公司平均，但從 4Q26 開始，對營益率有正向幫助， AI ASIC
業務的營益率高於公司平均，但由於其他業務有漲價，所以無
法量化差異。  
由於 4Q26 開始，營益率因 AI ASIC 業務已經開始改善，加上
相關營收將於 2027 年放量，聯發科認為 2027 年營益率至少能
在 20% 以上。

### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 2 日

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
環境保護：聯發科技做為全球半導體產業技術領導者，在整體價值鏈中位於
最前端的晶片設計，後端的原物料採購、代工製造、封裝與測試等環節皆交
由供應商執行，形成專業分工與互相合作的夥伴關係。根據風險調查與碳足
跡盤查發現，主要對社會與環境產生衝擊影響的關鍵在於後端製造作業，故
如何與供應商共同合作，以降低社會與環境相關的潛在風險，為聯發科技發
展綠色營運的首要任務。  
社會責任：運用數位科技解決社會問題不是企業的專利，聯發科技相信每個
人都有潛力利用數位科技創造無限可能，聯發科發起數位社會創新競賽 「智
在家鄉」，鼓勵社會大眾為家鄉問題提供解方。  
公司治理：聯發科技董事會下設有審計委員會，強化公司治理獨立性，並設
立薪資報酬委員會，以健全公司薪酬制度為目標，持續強化企業永續發展。
除前述法定功能性委員會外，本公司亦設立法定以外之併購策略委員會，視
實際需求針對集團具體併購案件及整體併購策略評估分析。  
3Q26 產品組合與說明：  
手機晶片占比 37% ，營收 QoQ-1%\YoY-17% ，因搭載 2nm 的天璣 9600 Pro
晶片的手機陸續上市，旗艦級產品表現優於其他產品。  
智慧裝置平台占比 58%，營收 QoQ+20%\YoY+63% ，因 5G 數據晶片導入平
板、 PC、車用座艙等裝置，並且 TV 晶片反映 DRAM 價格。天璣 9500 已搭
載於三星平板。  
PMIC 占比 5%，營收 QoQ+1%\YoY+18% ，因資料中心相關市占率提升。  
AI ASIC 業務的營益率高於公司平均：  
AI ASIC 業務的毛利率低於公司平均，但從 4Q26 開始，對營益率有正向幫
助， AI ASIC 業務的營益率高於公司平均，但由於其他業務有漲價，所以無
法量化差異。  
由於 4Q26 開始，營益率因 AI ASIC 業務已經開始改善，加上相關營收將於
2027 年放量，聯發科認為 2027 年營益率至少能在 20% 以上。  
聯發科第二個 AI ASIC 客戶與專案將於 2028 年開始貢獻營收：  
聯發科原先估第一個 AI ASIC 貢獻 4Q26 營收約 20 億美元，現在上修至貢
獻 25 億美元， 2027 年目標貢獻 160 億美元營收，為 AI ASIC 市場約 20% 市
佔。  
第二個 AI 加速器 ASIC 專案將於 2028 年初量產，此為新的非中系客戶，第
二個專案採用 Multi-die ，一些 die 已經 taped-out ，一些較大顆、較複雜的
die 會晚數周，仍符合時程。  
由於第二個 AI 加速器是採用 Multi-die ，合作對象為美國 IDM 與基板供應
商，聯發科會與台灣、日本供應商一起合作，由於供應鏈還有些變數，聯發
科暫時無法給出 2028 年相關業務的更多細節，目前只表示是非中系客戶。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 3

Hon Hai (2317.TW; Buy, on CL): New AI server racks and Foldable phones to drive 4Q26E

We expect Hon Hai’s monthly revenues to continue sequential growth through Dec 
2026, leading 4Q26 revenues to grow at +39% QoQ, driven by: (1) AI data center: The 
next-generation AI server racks and CPO switches ramp-up, with higher ASP and early 
delivery. We expect Hon Hai to be the first to deliver the latest model, around 2-3 
months ahead of peers, driving its Oct and onward revenue growth. Followed by 
GPU-powered AI server racks, the LPU-powered AI server racks should fuel the growth 
in Dec and onward, supporting the YoY increase in 1Q27. As our Global Server TAM 
update shows, we expect the AI server rack global value TAM to grow at 163% CAGR in 
2025-28E to US$1tn, or 63% of the global server value TAM by 2028E. And (2) 
Smartphone: riding on the major brand customer’s latest model launches along with the 
new form factor to attract more end users. As we highlighted in September (report link), 
we reduced our global smartphone volume estimates by 1m/ 8m/ 0.2m units to 1.1bn / 
1.2bn/ 1.2bn in 2026-28E, or -10% / +2%/ +2% YoY on high memory costs; however, we 
remain positive on the potential for global leading brands to outperform the market 
given their strong brand awareness, large procurement power, and customers’ stronger 
consumption power. We remain positive on foldable phones and expect premium 
models to continue to gain market share, which should be positive to Hon Hai. Maintain 
Buy (on CL).

3-month revenues preview: We expect the company’s 4Q26E revenues to be +61% 
YoY/ +39% QoQ to NT$4.2 trillion. 3Q26 delivered strong revenues YoY growth of 47%, 
or 5% / 7% ahead of our / Bloomberg consensus, and we expect to see both YoY and 
MoM growth throughout Oct-Dec, driven by new AI server racks, new smartphone 
models, and CPO switches. Management noted that AI-related products remain on track 
to maintain solid growth momentum through 4Q26, while ICT products continue to 
benefit from typical 2H seasonal tailwinds, which should support overall operational 
performance in line with current consensus expectations.

### Page 4

Price Target Risks and Methodology – Hon Hai 
Valuation methodology: We are Buy rated on Hon Hai. Our 12-month target price of 
NT$400 is based on an 17.9x 2027E P/E multiple, which is set in line with peers’ PEG&M 
ratio (i.e., P/E vs. forward-year earnings growth and OPM). 
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected ramp-up of the AI server business; (2) 
weaker-than-expected EV total solution performance across EV assembly, design, 
software, and semis; (3) slower-than-expected ramp-up of capacity globally; and (4) 
fiercer-than-expected competition in the consumer electronics EMS business.  
Quanta (2382.TW, Neutral): AI servers ramp-up to drive growth ahead; model transition and PC 
shipment slowdown could weigh on 4Q26

Quanta Sep revenues declined by 23% MoM, which we attributed to AI servers model 
transition, while PC entered quarter-end at a strong MoM of +62% MoM. Looking 
forward, we expect monthly revenues to resume sequential growth in Oct to Dec, riding 
on a reducing impact of AI servers model transition as new racks ramp up, along with 
growing general servers on agentic AI, and ASP increase on better specification and high 
raw material costs. We expect 4Q26E revenues to decline by 4% QoQ, considering the 
high base in 3Q26 and the gradual ramp-up of new AI server racks. As we highlighted in 
Oct (Global Server TAM updates), we expect global AI server racks (powered by NVIDIA 
and AMD) shipment at 63k / 110k / 164k in 2026E-28E along with the specification 
upgrade to drive the ASP; we raised leading US CSPs aggregate capex by 4% / 17% / 
22% in 2026E-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E, reflecting our positive view on AI spending.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Quanta 
We are Neutral rated on Quanta. Our 12-month target price of NT$300 is based on a 
10.7x 2027E P/E, which references PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple 
correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s performance. Key 
upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery; 2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers. 
Wiwynn (6669.TW, Buy): New ASIC and GPU AI server racks in expansion

Wiwynn delivered strong Sep revenues, which were 19% ahead of our estimates, 
reflecting strong general server demand from US leading CSPs, and its major customers’ 
new ASIC AI server ramping up. We expect revenue YoY growth to remain strong at mid 
double-digit through Dec 2026, leading 4Q26 revenues to grow at +60 YoY / +8% QoQ, 
driven by (1) major customers’ new ASIC AI servers ramping up, across air-cooled and 
liquid-cooled, (2) general servers end demand remaining solid on agentic AI, (3) new 
GPU-powered AI server racks entering mass production in 4Q26 across NVIDIA and 
AMD platforms, and (4) potential new customers in ASIC AI servers. As we highlighted in 
Oct (Global Server TAM update), we raised AI servers implied AI chips shipment by 9% / 
19% in 2027 / 28E with ASIC penetration rate increasing to 58% by 2028E, and our US 
internet team forecasts that leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will 
grow their capex budgets by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026-28E, with our US CSP capex 
estimates raised by 4% / 17% / 22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E, supporting 
our positive view on Wiwynn. Maintain Buy with an unchanged 12m TP of NT$4,000

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Wiwynn 
We are Buy-rated on Wiwynn with a 12-month TP of NT$4,000. Our target price is 
based on 20.3x 2027E P/E, which is derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E 
multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s performance. 
Key downside risks: 1) weaker-than-expected AI server demand growth; 2) 
slower-than-expected recovery in general server demand; and 3) more severe 
competition in the server ODM market.  
Wistron (3231.TW, Buy): AI server racks to see strong ramp-up with customer expansion and 
specification upgrades

Wistron’s Sep revenue was up 87% YoY to NT$381bn, or 18% lower than our estimate, 
which we attribute to the impact of shipment timing volatility in higher-ASP AI products. 
We expect sequential revenue growth ahead through Dec 2026, driven by new GPU AI 
server racks ramp-up in 4Q26, which should drive up the ASP , along with strong AI 
spending and expansion of the company’s customer base. The new GPU AI server racks 
have various racks across GPU, CPU, and LPU, supporting ODM growth in coming 
quarters. Sep PC shipment was up 7% MoM but down 32% YoY to 1.5m units, reflecting 
the slow PC demand amid the consumption pull-in under the high memory cost. Despite 
PC shipments growth likely being negative HoH considering the strong pull-in since late 
2025, we expect the strong AI server rack ramp-up to offset the impact, driving 2H26 
revenue up +41% HoH. Maintain Buy with an unchanged TP of NT$291 (12.6x 2027E P/E 
vs. 30.4% NI YoY in 2027-28E).

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Inventec  
We are Neutral-rated on Inventec with a 12-month target price of NT$61.0. Our target 
price is based on 14.2x 2027E P/E, which is derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth 
vs. P/E multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s 
performance. 
Key upside/downside risks: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market recovery; 
2) faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up; and 3) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected demand for general servers.

(2) broadband, IoT, and game console entering peak season. We are positive on

Maintain Sell on stretched valuation.

Pegatron’s Sep revenue was up 87% YoY / 143% MoM to NT$215bn, or 64% higher than 
our estimate. This reflects a strong ramp-up in AI servers in Sep. Sep PC shipments were 
up c.8% MoM but down 44% YoY to 0.46m, reflecting the weak PC demand under the 
high memory costs. Prior to September, the company suffered from model transition (i.e. 
declining demand for older models while new models were in a slow shipment ramp-up 
phase).

Earnings revision: We factor in Pegatron’s Sep revenue and raise our 2026-28E net 
income by 4% / 6% / 4%, mainly on higher revenues and lower opex ratio. We raise our 
2026-28E revenues by 16% / 28% / 27%, considering the AI server shipment ramp-up 
and higher ASP on better specification. Given the much lower GM of AI server racks 
compared to Pegatron’s other products, our changes in gross profits narrow to 9% / 12% 
/ 15% in 2026E-28E. The opex ratio for 2026-28E is cut by 0.1 ppt/ 0.2 ppt/ 0.1 ppt to 
reflect the improved operational efficiency.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

Idea
3Q26 earnings missed: Please see Exhibit 1 .
4Q guidance reflects weak NB demand: Parade is guiding rev. to decline 24% Q/Q
at mid-point, 32% below consensus. We overestimated the stability of demand from
standard-plus customers, while demand from standard customers remains very
weak, as expected. Based on the current demand outlook into 1Q27, it is difficult to
see a V-shaped recovery. That said, we appreciate Parade's supply chain
management and its ability to pass cost pressures on to customers. We see Parade's

TTED business offering L T opportunities amid PCB price hikes.
New business ramp could take time: We previously modelled the company’s ASIC-
like business contributing 15-20% of revenue in 2027-28. Given the complexity of
the chip design and weak DT demand, we believe we were too optimistic. As for the
TTED business, the company expects shipments to exceed 5mn units in 2026, which
could account for ~8% of 2026 revenue. The company expects NB shipments to

remain similar in 2027 vs. 2026, subject to DRAM pricing. Currently, we do not
expect DRAM pricing to decline meaningfully.
Double downgrade to UW; PT NT$444: We cut our earnings forecasts mainly to
reflect weaker 4Q guidance and a lower contribution from the ASIC-like business.
We lowered 2026/27/28e earnings by 14%/39%/43%. The company is now trading at
17x 2027e EPS. We like Parade's long-term growth potential but would need to see a
meaningful resumption in business growth to turn more positive. (continued on the

next page)

3Q26 earnings review: 3Q revenue was NT$4.2bn, -2% Q/Q, -4% Y/Y, and 4% above MSe; 
3Q GM was 40.3%, flattish Q/Q, -2.2ppt Y/Y and 2.3ppt below MSe; 3Q EPS was NT$7.67, 
flat Q/Q, -27% Y/Y and 6% below MSe. Weaker 2H26 demand has been due to OEM pull-
ins in 1H, compressed consumer demand due to price hikes, and compressed enterprise 
budgets due to allocation to AI spending.
Exhibit 1: 3Q26 earnings review
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research (E) estimates
Why it's not too late to downgrade now: The weak 4Q26 outlook lowers the earnings 
base, while visibility on a sustained recovery remains limited. With NB shipments expected 
to remain broadly flat in 2027, TTED growth and initial ASIC-like contributions may be 
insufficient to offset weakness in the core business. The risk of prolonged earnings 
pressure and a slower-than-expected ramp in new businesses supports our more cautious 
view beyond the near-term reset.

Parade: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target from  NT$818 to NT$444:  Our price target is our base case
scenario value, which we continue to derive from a residual income model. Our bull and
bear case scenario values are cut from NT$948 to NT$755, and from NT$418 to NT$288,
respectively.
Intermediate growth cut to 9.4% from 11.4% to reflect slower ramp up of new businesses.
Other key RI assumptions are unchanged:
• 9.8% cost of equity (beta 1.3, risk-free rate 2% and risk premium 6%)

• Payout ratio of 50%
• Terminal growth rate of 4.0%

STOCK RATINGS

Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
(-) PC OEMs and ODMs: Intel (covered by Joseph Moore) expects 2H26 PC units to
fall 20%+ y/y as OEMs aggressively cut inventories amid weaker sell-through; the
correction has accelerated over the past two months. This aligns with Morgan

Stanley's estimate at a ~25% y/y decline  (report link).
(+) Server OEMs and ODMs: Intel guides for server CPU unit growth of 30%+ in
both 2026 and 2027, with upside if wafer/substrate supply improves. It sees server
CPU TAM rising from US$40bn today to >US$200bn in five years, driven mainly by
AI agents.
(+) ABF substrates:  Most of the previously discussed ~40% capacity increase is due
in 2H27; Intel expects key component purchase commitments to rise sharply over

the next several quarters. Intel is also prioritizing securing substrate capacity and
improving yields for the EMIB-T. Complex designs add yield risk, which could lower
effective output, worsening ABF substrate shortages but adding further support for
pricing.

Key Takeaways
We view negative PC comments as more bearish for OEMs and ODMs positioned
in the lower end consumer segment, like Acer and Compal.
Strong CPU unit growth would be positive for Wiwynn, Lenovo, and Lotes.
Tighter ABF substrate supply and stronger pricing would be positive for
Unimicron and NYPCB's revenue and margins.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Document 167: 261103_daiwa_Realtek

Source PDF: all report pdf/261103_daiwa_Realtek.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following our results memo, we fine-tune our forecasts for 
Realtek. We believe its near-term demand-rebalancing hiccups have come 
to an end, thanks to its pricing power and its ability to pass on supply-chain 
cost inflation. Given our interpretation of management’s tone that 4Q26 is 
likely to be sequentially flat, we expect Realtek’s business to return to its 
seasonal track from 1Q27. We expect it to continue capitalising on its 
structure growth driver of multi-year/multi-G (gigabits per second) 
connectivity bandwidth upgrade cycle, with any AI penetration adding to 
upside. We reaffirm Outperform (2) call with a higher 12M TP of TWD798.

What’s the impact: Forecast revisions. We fine-tune our 2026-28E EPS 
given that fundamentals are broadly intact heading into 2027. Recall 
management’s commentary at its results call (29 October) of expecting 
4Q26 to be a “prudent and steady” quarter. We interpret this as likely a 
sequentially flat revenue, counter-seasonal after its 3Q26 miss on deeper 
demand rebalancing. Without quantifying its 2027 business outlook, 
management expects the year to be one of steady growth. We forecast 
Realtek’s 2027 revenue to grow by 18% YoY, assuming its business returns 
to seasonal track after 2 years of demand rebalancing hiccups caused by 
customers’ unusual restocking and pre-stocking activities.

Back on track after recent hiccups. After 2 years of anti-seasonal 
performance on customer pre-stocking amid uncertainty over Trump’s 
reciprocal tariffs, followed by component price hikes and supply-chain 
uncertainty related to AI cannibalisation, we expect Realtek’s business to 
return to its seasonal track from 1Q27. We believe it should continue to 
capitalise on its secular driver of multi-year/multi-G bandwidth upgrade 
cycle from both wireline and wireless connectivity, including 2.5G/5G/10G 
Ethernet, 10GPON, managed switches and WiFi 7/8. Its penetration into AI, 
from cloud AI companion chips to edge AI to physical AI, should add to 
growth upside, although any meaningful contribution may take time, 
evidenced by its new product offering/development such as SSD, Ethernet 
hub, optical cable, i3C security and SerDes controllers, to name a few. 
According to management, AI will contribute a high single-digit % revenue 
this year, ramping to over 30% contribution by 2030.

What we recommend: Following forecast revisions, we raise our 12M TP 
for Realtek to TWD798 (previous: TWD750), on the same 4-quarter forward 
PER target of 20x. We reaffirm our Outperform (2) rating with key downside 
risk of any component price hikes leading to worse-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are broadly in line with consensus, 
ranging from -3% to +2%.

3 November 2026

Back on track after rebalancing

➢ Fine-tuning our forecasts on demand rebalancing after pre-stocking 
➢ Likely back on seasonal track after this year’s hiccups 
➢ Reaffirming Outperform (2) call with a higher 12-month TP of TWD798

Company profile 
Realtek focuses on research, design, development and distribution of integrated circuits (ICs) for 
applications across networking & communication (NC), computer & peripheral (CP) and multimedia & 
consumer (MM). The majority of its revenue comes from NC products, including WiFi, Bluetooth, 
Ethernet and switch controllers. It also provides diversified CP products such as audio codec, IP cam, 
and solid-state drive (SSD) controllers as well as MM products like TV SoC and monitor controllers.

### Page 5

5

Realtek Semiconductor (2379 TT): 3 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 29 Jan 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Realtek’s Board of Directors consists of nine members whose expertise spans industry,
academia, finance, accounting, and management, providing the company with a broad and
diverse range of professional perspectives. Three of these directors are independent,
representing 33 percent of the board and exceeding the regulatory minimum requirement of 20
percent for independent directors. This higher proportion of independent directors enhances
oversight, helps safeguard shareholder interests, and supports more objective decision‑making.
However, an important governance risk remains in the area of board diversity. The board
currently includes only one female director out of nine members, which represents 11 percent of
the total. This limited female representation may constrain the breadth of viewpoints and
experiences reflected in board discussions and could be perceived as misaligned with evolving
stakeholder expectations and best practices on gender diversity in corporate governance.
Capital management 1
In the past 8 years, Realtek's dividend payout ratios were above 80%. As a fabless company, it
does not need high capital expenditure for manufacturing sites. Realtek's business has shown
stable earnings growth over its 30-year history. Thus, we see its dividend policy with a high
payout ratio as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 2
Realtek’s related-party transactions are primarily with G.M.I. Technology Inc. and Greatek
Electronics Inc. Realtek pays certain processing costs to related parties. These payments are
made under standard commercial terms and conditions. Overall, we do not observe any
significant impact on Realtek’s business arising from these related-party transactions.
S Supply chainmanagement 2
Realtek has formulated a set of supplier management regulations in accordance with Realtek's
Supplier Sustainability Management Policy. It requires suppliers to obtain ISO9001 quality
management system certification to ensure that all of its suppliers possess a high level of
environment awareness.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 1
As a fabless company without manufacturing plants, Realtek does not produce much wastewater
compared to manufacturing companies, and thus has a relatively limited impact on the
environment. Realtek has implemented a water conservation plan to increase water efficiency,
such as the reuse of rainwater, re-circulating water in its cooling tower and installing water-
saving devices. Its water saving rate (=reduced water consumption/total water withdrawn)
increased gradually.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
The company reports to the Executive Yuan's Environmental Protection Administration waste
and reporting management system every month on the quantity of waste and its method of
disposal. In 2024, it produced 26.10 tonnes of industrial waste, maintaining over a 90% recycling
rate for hazardous industrial waste.
E GHG emissions 1
Realtek has independently conducted GHG emission inventory and management at its Hsinchu
Headquarters since 2019. In 2022, Realtek implemented a comprehensive ISO 14064-1:2018
GHG inventory and passed third-party verification. As of 2024, the company has completed
GHG emission inventory and third-party verification for all sites within the Realtek Group.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

公司簡介  
富世達股份有限公司成立於 2001 年 ， 專精於
機械運動結構件之設計與製造 ， 主要產品包括
轉軸、滑軌、以及作動於轉軸與滑軌之結構件
等 ， 應用範圍方面則以 3C 產品為主 ， 並以折
疊式手機及高端筆記型電腦等利基型市場為
大宗， 持續深耕戴爾 、 聯想 、 華為等主力客戶 。
近幾年 亦隨著母公司奇鋐科技 將觸角延伸至
伺服器水冷接頭、滑軌等部件領域，業務成功
擴展至雲端業者和 AI 模型開發商。

2

營運展望 
1. 3Q26 伺服器相關新產品開始放量帶動營運重新增溫 ：產品組合持續優化，伺
服器營收比重已由去年同期的五成多拉升至目前的八成以上 ， 帶動毛利率走揚 。
營業費用增加主因持續擴編研發團隊並加速新專案推進，致使研發支出攀升。
富世達第三季營收 45.0 億元、+20.4% QoQ、+26.2% YoY，毛利率 38.0%。營
業利益 14.6 億元、+39.9% QoQ、+84.2% YoY，營業利益率 32.4%。歸屬母公
司稅後淨利  12.4 億元，每股盈餘  18.12 元。產品組合分別為 摺疊手機轉軸  
16% (-53% YoY)、伺服器液冷快接頭和滑軌 81% (+92% YoY)、筆電轉軸 3% (-
27% YoY)、其他 1% (+24% YoY)。 
2. 伺服器液冷快接頭和滑軌隨著新品放量營收貢獻大幅增加：第三季伺服器液冷
快接頭和滑軌佔公司整體營收比重 81.3% 約 36.6 億元、+78.1% QoQ、+91.7% 
YoY。伺服器產品線今年營收維持翻倍增長，在手專案量充沛，明年度營運將
持續維持高度成長格局。AWS Trainium3 伺服器滑軌已正式取得客戶端認證，
出貨時程將直接對接客戶與系統整合廠的建置節奏。水冷快接頭產能由原先規
劃的單月三百萬顆顯著拉升五成至四百五十萬顆規模；產能稼動率緊密配合下
游客戶拉貨排程逐步走揚。伺服器滑軌目前產能維持單月三十五萬套規模，後
續新產線擴建將依各大專案最終驗證過測進度彈性調度。伺服器產品毛利率顯
著優於傳統手機轉軸業務；受惠高毛利伺服器營收佔比擴大，疊加製造端自動
化與製程持續優化，整體綜合毛利率維持穩健向上走勢。 
3. 摺疊手機軸承單價因規格提升持續拉高但出貨節奏受干擾：第三季摺疊手機轉
軸佔公司整體營收比重 15.5% 約 7.0 億元、-51.1% QoQ、-52.7% YoY。配合
客戶下半年旗艦新機需求，軸承組件已正式啟動出貨；惟短期出貨節奏受記憶
體零組件缺料干擾，整體拉貨動能出現些微遞延。規格演進明確朝向更輕薄化
架構邁進；因應機構強度與可靠度的高標準要求，下一代產品已積極導入新材
質與新配方進行研發，以滿足高剛性需求。 
4. 筆電轉軸事業逐漸淡出：第三季筆電轉軸佔公司整體營收比重 2.6% 約 1.2 億
元、-49.8% QoQ、-27.2% YoY。受到中國同業削價競爭 ， 此產品線毛利率承壓 ，
未來營運貢獻將持續降低。 
5. 產品組合持續優化，但適逢各家平台轉換空窗期：隨著高階伺服器的液冷快接
頭和滑軌出貨持續成長，產品組合大幅優化，預期今明年營運獲利將創歷史新
高。合庫預估 2026 年營收 179.3 億元、+44.4% YoY，毛利率 36.4%。營業利
益 55.3 億元、+118.9% YoY，營業利益率 30.8%。歸屬母公司稅後淨利 45.9 
億元，每股盈餘 66.98 元。 
6. ESG (富世達在 2026 年上市公司治理評鑑結果為 51 ~ 65%)：富世達成立以
來，將永續經營承諾自「環境保護」 「社會責任」及「公司治理」三面向著手進

### Page 3

TAIWAN COOPERATIVE SECURITIES

3

行，在致力追求企業成長及永續經營之際，始終以「誠信經營」貫徹於產業鏈
上下游與客戶及供應商維持穩定良好的合作關係，並藉由各項企業社會公益活
動的參與，善盡企業社會責任貢獻一份心力。

評等標準說明 
評等 定義 
買進 預期未來 6 個月潛在報酬率>15% 
區間 預期未來 6 個月潛在報酬率 0%~15% 
中立 預期未來 6 個月潛在報酬率<0% 
未評等 訊息不足或有相關業務往來

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련 
sally.kim@ls-sec.co.kr

Document 340: Czech National Bank review_ The new neutral normal

Source PDF: all report pdf/Czech National Bank review_ The new neutral normal.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

The CNB increased the policy rate to 4%, in line with market
expectations. However, the decision was very close, a 4-3 split.
The new CNB forecast is revised toward a punchier inﬂation
amid a softer economic expansion, implying a higher rates
trajectory. We see 4% as the new normal, while further hikes
will be restrained by dwindling economic activity

David Havrlant and Frantisek Taborsky
In a split 4-3 vote, the Czech National Bank raised its main interest rate by
25bp to 4.00%
Punchier inﬂation amid mediocre economic
performance
The CNB proceeded with a rate hike on Thursday, raising the base
rate to 4%, which matches the expectations of market participants.
The decision was taken in a split vote as four board members
favoured a tighter monetary policy setup, while three policymakers
opted for rate stability. The situation is perceived as inﬂationary,
with persistently elevated core inﬂation, weaker exchange rate, and
a still strong housing market, along with ample credit growth, seen

as the main forces to keep in check through a tighter monetary
policy stance. Robust wage growth and expansive ﬁscal policy are
also assessed as risks to price stability. The decision and the mood
of the press conference are in line with our expectations in both
respects: i) one rate rise in November, and ii) concerns about
economic performance putting a lid on further tightening.
CNB's real GDP path remains on the
optimistic side
Source: CNB, ING, Macrobond

The new CNB staﬀ projection points to slower economic expansion
along with more elevated inﬂation, as we have foreseen. We must
notice with some delight that the CNB inﬂation outlook is quite
close to ING's view throughout the forecast horizon. Meanwhile, the
CNB growth outlook remains a little optimistic compared with our
view, especially for the coming year. As for short-term interest rate,
the CNB forecast suggests a further increase up to 4.5% in the

nearest future, followed by a gradual decline from 2Q27 to 3.5% at
the end of 2028. We see such a trajectory rather as an alternative
scenario, should inﬂation prove even stronger than in our forecast.
Forecast marriage in inﬂation

### Page 3

Source: CNB, ING, Macrobond
The interesting thing is that the governor described the monetary
conditions as very tight at this point, and even beforehand. Well,
should our hypothesis about the need for much higher equilibrium
rates in the brave new world be right, then 4% seems to be a
neutral stance that just prevents inﬂation from systematically
hovering above the target. And with the CNB rate projection landing
at 3.5% at the very end of the forecasting horizon, we believe that

the staﬀ is perhaps starting to drift in the same direction, at least
implicitly.
That said, interest rates are an endogenous variable in the CNB
workhorse model, so you can argue that this particular rate path
enables the 2.5% economic expansion, with inﬂation receding
towards the target. But hey, would you really be cutting rated so
swiftly, while having inﬂation still running above your target and
the economy growing around its potential? I have some doubts,

but will stick to the old good maxim in dubio pro reo.
The bank board will likely smooth out the
rates path

### Page 4

Source: CNB, ING, Macrobond
The infamous “all options are open” mantra was brought out of its
shallow grave and into the daylight once again, but the dwindling
real interest rate in the outlook does not suggest it stands on solid
ground. Overall, we see 4% as the new neutral normal and believe
that this will enable the CNB to ride out the wave of second-round
eﬀects, provided that our real GDP and inﬂation forecasts are right.
Our market view
The main takeaway from the CNB meeting is the 4–3 split

within the board, following a long period of strong majorities.
The market interprets this as a dovish signal, suggesting the
CNB is unwilling to hike rates further unless the inﬂation
situation deteriorates signiﬁcantly. For the ﬁrst time since the
onset of the US-Iran conﬂict, the CNB’s forecast aligns closely
with market pricing, with the PRIBOR rate peaking at around
4.55%. However, inﬂation is expected to worsen over the
remainder of the year, approaching 3% and nearing 4% in

the ﬁrst quarter of 2027, bringing the new CNB outlook closer
to our own forecast. We do not believe the current shift in
energy prices justiﬁes such a rally in CZK rates. The move
appears excessive at this juncture. Consequently, we view
two additional rate hikes as the minimum the market will

EUR/CZK is facing renewed upward pressure. Indeed, if we

take the shift in rate diﬀerentials at face value, the rates point
toward 24.450. However, as previously noted, rates are likely
to correct, as the current rate diﬀerential is too tight.
Furthermore, the koruna beneﬁts from strong seasonal
support in November and December, which should help
temper the current weakening pressure following the dovish
CNB meeting. We therefore anticipate a new range of 24.350–
24.400, little changed from our pre-meeting expectations.

Document 352: ESG与可持续发展周报：企业可持续披露体系持续完善，进一步健全气候准则配套应用体系

Source PDF: all report pdf/ESG与可持续发展周报：企业可持续披露体系持续完善，进一步健全气候准则配套应用体系.pdf
Report source: 上海证券
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

证
券
研
究
报
告ESG
与
可
持
续
发
展
周
报
企业可持续披露体系持续完善，进一步健全气
候准则配套应用体系
——ESG 与可持续发展周报（20261026-1101）
[Table_Summary] 主要观点
 政策解读
2026 年 9 月 28 日 ，财 政部 发 布《 〈企 业 可持 续 披露 准则 第 1
号——气候（试行）〉应用指南（征求意见稿）》及电力、钢铁、煤
炭、石油、化肥、铝、氢、水泥、汽车等 9 项行业应用指南（征求意
见稿），意见反馈截止日期为 2026 年 10 月 26 日。
2024 年 11 月，财政部会同外交部、国家发改委、工业和信息化
部、生态环境部、商务部、中国人民银行、国务院国资委、金融监管
总局、中国证监会（以下简称 9 部门）制定了《企业可持续披露准
则——基本准则（试行）》（以下简称基本准则），拉开了国家统一
的可持续披露准则体系建设的序幕。2025 年 12 月，财政部会同 9 部
门制定了气候准则，标志着国家统一的可持续披露准则体系建设从宏

观框架向具体议题纵深推进。
国家统一的可持续披露准则体系由基本准则、具体准则和应用指
南构成。准则应用指南和行业应用指南相互衔接、各有侧重，共同为
企业贯彻实施气候准则提供配套指引。其中，准则应用指南主要聚焦
企业执行气候准则过程种的共性问题，负责对气候准则部分条款进行
解释说明；行业应用指南主要聚焦行业特定事项，侧重于行业特定风
险，机遇和排放源识别，以及指标和目标设定。
准则应用指南共五章。第一章关于气候相关影响信息的披露。对
气候准则中气候相关影响信息的披露流程进行说明，给出识别、分
析、确认和披露四个步骤的具体操作方法。第二章为关于气候相关战
略信息的披露。对气候准则战略方面的披露要求进行细化和示例，包
括气候相关风险和机遇的识别与披露、风险和机遇对企业战略和决策
影响的披露要求、当期和预期财务影响的分析步骤、气候韧性评估等
方面的技术指引。第三章关于气候相关风险和机遇管理信息的披露。
对气候准则风险和机遇管理方面的披露要求进行细化和示例，包括风
险管理流程、机遇管理流程、风险和机遇管理流程融入企业整体风险

管理流程的融合程度与融合路径。第四章为关于温室气体排放信息的
披露。对气候准则中行业通用指标“温室气体排放”的分类、核算流
程和核算方法进行介绍。第五章为关于行业特定指标的披露。对气候
准则中行业特定指标的披露要求进行说明，与行业应用指南实现衔
接。
各行业应用指南在框架上保持一致。第一章为行业介绍。明确各
项行业应用指南的适用范围，并结合行业价值链特征以及在应对气候
变化中面临的实际场景，阐述行业气候相关信息披露的背景。第二章
为行业特定气候相关信息。梳理行业特定的气候相关风险和机遇，以
及企业活动对经济、环境和社会产生的气候相关影响。第三章为行业
特定气候相关风险和机遇的财务影响。针对各行业识别的重点气候相
关风险和机遇，列示其对财务状况、经营成果和现金流量的当期和预
期财务影响。第四章为行业温室气体排放。结合各行业生产工艺和排
放特征，列举各行业常见温室气体排放源。第五章为行业特定指标和
目标。结合各行业特点与实际，设置行业特定指标和目标。

ESG 与可持续发展周报
露准则 第 1 号——气候（试行）》答记者问时表示：综合考虑我国企
业的发展阶段和披露能力，将坚持准则制定和实施分开的原则，实事
求是、稳中求进，沿用《基本准则》的实施安排，不采取“一刀切”
的强制实施要求，现阶段将《气候准则》定位为试行文件，明确在实
施范围和实施要求作出规定之前，由企业自愿实施。后续将采取区分
重点、试点先行、循序渐进、分步推进的策略，从上市公司向非上市
公司扩展，从大型企业向中小企业扩展，从定性要求向定量要求扩
展，从自愿披露向强制披露扩展。
我们认为，指南的制定发布，标志着气候准则配套应用体系进一
步健全，对规范我国企业气候相关信息披露、引导企业践行可持续发
展理念、完善国家统一的可持续披露准则体系具有重要意义。强化了
支持绿色低碳发展的标准供给，有助于引导市场预期、规范企业行
为、科学评估转型进展，为“双碳”目标从国家宏观战略向企业微观
行动转化提供了关键的政策工具与制度基础设施。
 行业动态
（1）自然资源部 28 日发布消息，前三季度，我国海洋经济总体保持

平稳，发展态势向新向优，发展韧性持续彰显。前三季度，海洋经济
总量保持平稳增长。初步核算，前三季度我国海洋生产总值 8.5 万亿
元，同比增长 4.9%，高于国内生产总值增速 0.3 个百分点。海洋经济
引擎作用持续释放。作为国民经济重要增长极，海洋经济拉动国民经
济增长 0.4 个百分点。海洋资源要素保障机制不断完善。海域资源要
素配置改革加快向纵深推进，积极探索海域分层立体利用，优化提升
海域资源管理水平。
（2）近日，交通运输部等八部门联合印发《综合交通枢纽发展“十五
五”规划》（简称《规划》），聚焦提升国内外、干支线、跨方式衔
接转换效率，着力提能级、强连接、增质效，统筹推进综合交通枢纽
集群、枢纽城市、枢纽港站建设，打造旅客便捷换乘中心和物流高效
集散中心。《规划》将安全、智慧、绿色理念贯穿枢纽建设运营，要
求提高综合客运枢纽安全应急能力，提升综合货运枢纽安全韧性；完
善综合客运枢纽电子客票服务，加强不同运输方式票务系统的有效衔
接，推进综合货运枢纽仓储、运输、配送等设施设备智能化和自动化

升级；依托国家综合交通运输信息平台，推动各种运输方式信息共
享，提高枢纽港站数据资源的采集与汇聚能力；推动综合客运枢纽绿
色化转型，打造零碳综合货运枢纽，推动新能源重卡在枢纽应用，推
动物流设施设备“油改电”。
 技术前沿
近日，清华大学碳中和研究院工业深度减碳研究中心双聘教师、
清华大学化工系宝丰碳中和讲席教授唐军旺团队在甲醇绿色转化领域
取得新进展。团队提出光子-声子协同驱动催化策略，通过 Li 调控 Pt
单原子局域结构，实现了温和条件下甲醇高选择性制甲醛并副产氢
气。
该研究将体相光物理过程与表面化学反应相衔接，通过光子与声
子 分 别 满 足 不 同 的 反 应 步 骤 所 需 的 能 量 （ 也 即 能 量 的 量 化 供 应 概
念），为克服单一能量场下的效率与选择性限制提供了新思路，也为
低温甲醛绿色合成及联产绿氢提供了工业放大基础。研究成果以“甲
醇绿色合成氢气与甲醛的量子效率增强研究”（Green synthesis of
hydrogen and formaldehyde from methanol with enhanced quantum

efficiency ） 为 题 ， 于 10 月 5 日 发 表 于 《 自 然 · 可 持 续 发 展 》
（Nature Sustainability）。

### Page 3

ESG 与可持续发展周报
 研究探索
2026 年 10 月 21 日，在 2026 金融街论坛年会“融智·融绿·融
新——金融创新构建未来可持续投资新生态”分论坛上，中国社会科
学院金融研究所研究员、国家金融与发展实验室理事张跃文发布《中
国金融创新与可持续投资发展报告》。当前资本大量涌向人工智能等
短期商业回报显著的赛道，而周期长、规模大、收益不确定的可持续
投资领域却存在大量的资金缺口，在目前这个阶段，这两类投资一定
程度上形成了资本争夺。
报告显示，我国可持续投资的总体发展态势较好，广义责任投资
规模已经超过了 50 万亿元，其中绿色信贷大概占 90%的比重，与此
同时绿色产业基金、ESG 股权基金、绿色债券、绿色 REITs 等工具不
断丰富，已经初步形成了覆盖多种工具的多层次可持续投资的市场体
系，绿色金融和科技金融的协同效益开始显现，为未来产业注入动
能。但基于周期长、收益滞后；不确定性高；价值维度多元化，社会
效益与经济效益不完全同步；不同阶段的融资需求差异大；信息识别

复杂这五大特征。当前可持续投资也相应暴露出五大薄弱环节：长期
资本供给不足，保险等长期资金对项目投资收益和现金流确定性有较
高要求；缺乏适配项目的个性化金融产品；行业缺少成熟的估值定价
体系；各类金融工具之间缺少平滑衔接的过渡产品；同时专业人才缺
口大，懂绿色环保的不一定懂金融，懂金融的对绿色环保的认识可能
又不够深入。报告研究通过金融创新去解决目前可持续投资所存在的
问题，但这个金融创新不仅包括金融产品和服务的创新，也包括金融
运行模式、金融生态、金融监管规则的创新。要围绕估值定价、信息
识别、期限匹配、风险分担、资产盘活五大作用机制发力。
 国际合作
2026 年 10 月 26 日，“一带一路”绿色创新大会在香港举办。会
议以“携手南方绿色转型，共促全球可持续发展”为主题，由生态环
境部与香港特别行政区政府环境及生态局共同主办，中心与“一带一
路”绿色发展国际联盟（以下简称绿色联盟）、香港特别行政区政府
环境保护署共同承办。生态环境部部长黄润秋、香港特别行政区行政
长官李家超出席开幕式并致辞，来自 20 余个国家及地区和国际组织的

近 300 名代表参会。会议期间，中心主任、绿色联盟副理事长张志锋
主持“专题对话二：共促绿色低碳创新技术与产业协同”，与共建
“一带一路”国家环境主管部门、国际组织和科研机构代表，围绕全
球南方绿色低碳创新技术与产业合作进行研讨。
 投资建议
我们认为，《〈企业可持续披露准则第 1 号——气候（试行）〉
应用指南（征求意见稿）》的制定发布，标志着气候准则配套应用体
系进一步健全，对规范我国企业气候相关信息披露、引导企业践行可
持续发展理念、完善国家统一的可持续披露准则体系具有重要意义。
强化了支持绿色低碳发展的标准供给，有助于引导市场预期、规范企
业行为、科学评估转型进展，为“双碳”目标从国家宏观战略向企业
微观行动转化提供了关键的政策工具与制度基础设施。
 风险提示
ESG相关政策推行不及预期；市场情绪与偏好波动风险等。

Economy 
Insurance costs hit the brakes 
16 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Property insurance (e.g., auto, homeowners, renters) payment growth has slowed considerably, according to Bank of America 
payments data. Median payments rose just 1% year-over-year (YoY) in 2026, easing further from the 6% growth seen in 2025, 
after a roughly 12% increase in 2024, suggesting that many consumers are getting some relief from rising insurance payments. 
• However, the moderation is uneven across consumers. Bank of America payments data suggests that Gen Z continues to 
experience insurance payment growth of nearly 6%, while increases for other generations are closer to 1% to 2%. Payments 
also rose nearly 1.5% for higher-income households but edged lower for those with lower incomes. 
• Lower accident rates, easing vehicle prices, and softer major-purchase growth may be reducing cost pressures, but geography 
remains a major dividing line. Florida metros recorded declines of roughly 7% to 8%, while California and several Northeastern 
metros continued to see relatively strong increases.

Insurance payments stabilize after years of painful growth 
The burden of higher property (e.g., auto, renters, homeowners) insurance payments may be easing for some. Notably, while the 
average payment increased 3% year-over-year (YoY), the median payment was only up 1%, according to Bank of America 
payments data. This suggests that most consumers have only seen a small increase in their premiums, though there may be a 
few outliers (Exhibit 1).  
The latest data stands in contrast to just two years ago in 2024, when most consumers saw their insurance payments surge 
about 12% YoY, after a nearly 8% YoY increase in 2023.   
Exhibit 1: Insurance payments have increased only modestly through August of this year, with growth peaking in 2024 
Median and average insurance payments (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Insurance payments by age show a similar pattern, with much stronger growth in 2024 across most generations followed by a 
cooling since then (Exhibit 2). Gen Z is the outlier here, with their payments still growing at a healthy rate, increasing almost 6% 
YoY for both 2026 and 2025, after a 7% increase in 2024. Meanwhile, Millennials and Baby Boomers experienced a 2% YoY 
increase so far this year, with Gen X only seeing around a 1% rise.

Bank of America payments data by income provides even more insight. Higher-income households paid nearly 1.5% more in 
2026 than they did a year ago, while middle-income households saw only modest increases or none at all (Exhibit 3). Conversely, 
lower-income households actually paid slightly less so far this year than they did last year.  
Exhibit 3: Higher-income households are still paying more for insurance compared to last year, while lower earners are paying less 
Median insurance payments by income cuts (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Fewer accidents and lower vehicles prices may be driving insurance payments down 
But why might insurance payments be declining at all? One thing to keep in mind is that insurance companies often raise rates 
to cover events that occurred in previous years to recover costs, while adjusting for changing risks.  
For example, the crash rate – the number of vehicle crashes per one million miles driven – suggests that there was a sharp rise 
in accidents in 2021, according to the Department of Transportation (Exhibit 4). Additionally, new and used vehicle prices 
increased significantly after the pandemic, according to Consumer Price Index (CPI) data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics 
(BLS) (Exhibit 5). So, insurers were not only facing more vehicle replacements due to a higher crash rate, but a higher cost to 
replace vehicles as well.

However, the crash rate mostly declined from 2021 to 2024, and the Department of Transportation reported that the number of 
accidents likely decreased again in 2025. Also, while the cost to maintain and repair vehicles has consistently grown since 2021, 
the price to replace vehicles peaked in 2022 and has eased since then – the latter likely a more important factor in insurance 
costs than the former. Additionally, while the CPI index for motor vehicle insurance has fallen 5% YoY, it’s possible that this may 
partially be due to rebates, according to BofA Global Research. So, the price figured into CPI may not be fully realized by the 
consumer.

Another factor in the divergence between a declining CPI and rising insurance payments in our data may be the growth in the 
amount of property people are insuring now. Notably though, this growth has eased in recent years. YoY growth in both new 
home and total vehicle sales peaked in mid-2023 and has grown modestly, or even declined, since (Exhibit 6). And given that 
these major purchases are still K-shaped (read more in Autos: In gear, but the “K” is still in the headlights and On the move: 
Renovation over relocation?), it could help to explain the divergence in insurance payment growth across income cohorts.

Florida’s metros have seen the largest decline when compared to other major cities 
Examining different cities across the US also sheds light on insurance trends. Consumers in major California metros saw the 
largest increases in 2026, following near double digit growth in 2025, likely driven by widespread property damage from 
wildfires, according to BofA Global Research (Exhibit 7). Major coastal metros in the Northeast like Boston and New York also 
experienced sizable increases as well.  
Conversely, many cities throughout the Sun Belt like Houston, Nashville and Dallas saw flat or even declining insurance 
payments. BofA Global Research points to several reasons for these declines: part of the equation in this region is that the 
massive runup in the cost of property insurance and domestic migration to the region through 2024 has since eased or reversed 
course.   
Exhibit 7: California major metros have seen steady insurance payment growth, while Miami metros have seen declines 
Median insurance payments by core based statistical areas (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
In Florida, legislation that went into effect in 2023, eventually made its way into insurance prices in 2025, leading to widespread 
declines in premiums. The state also has a “lookback period” of three years, where insurance profits can be deemed too high and 
a rebate is issued to consumers. It’s possible this rebate is factoring into declines this year, as some carriers only allow rebates 
upon renewals.  
The Sunshine State was a notable standout for declines, with Orlando, Tampa and Jacksonville each down around 8% YoY in 
2026, followed closely by Miami with a 7% decrease. Additionally, Bank of America payments data suggests that Jacksonville, 
Miami and Orlando all had median insurance payments above the national level in 2023, though their payments were fell below 
that in 2026 (Exhibit 8).  
Major metros in Texas also saw easing insurance payments relative to 2023, while Seattle, San Jose, and Riverside, CA are 
among the major metros that went from below the national median to above in the three-year period ending in 2026.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Insurance payments include renters, auto or home, but do not include insurance escrowed into mortgage payments or deducted from a 
paycheck.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Key points – September quarter 2026
• The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in labour market conditions. The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an
almost five-year run of increases. The unemployment rate is now at its highest level since September 2015.
• There were mixed messages in terms of jobs growth: the Household Labour Force Survey reported a 0.1% fall in employment, while the employer-

focused Quarterly Employment Survey suggested a modest rise. The Monthly Employment Indicator had also pointed to a small rise in jobs.
• The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in the participation rate. These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly large
increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error in the survey rather than a
genuine surge.
• We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message here is one of softness. Our

best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population.
• While the economy has returned to growth over the last year or so, hiring activity remains weak. This may be an ongoing legacy of employment trends in
previous years, when firms were relatively reluctant to lay people off during the slowdown, which then potentially left them overstaffed even as activity
started to pick up again. It’s not clear how long this effect can persist, though.

• Wage growth remains muted. Although cost-of-living pressures have increased on workers, the existing degree of spare capacity in the labour market
doesn’t give them much leverage to negotiate larger pay increases.
• Overall, we judge these results to be weaker than the RBNZ expected, in particular the rise in the unemployment rate. This data supports the views of
those on the policy committee who were less convinced in October about the potential for second-round inflation pressures in the economy.

### Page 3

Westpac New Zealand Economics
3
Employment
• The number of people employed fell by 0.1% in the September quarter, according to the
Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS). Previous quarters were revised down a little,
reflecting downward revisions to the working-age population estimates. Annual employment
growth slowed from 0.9% to 0.7%.
• This was substantially softer than our forecast of a 0.5% rise for the quarter. Our forecast
was based on the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI), a comprehensive measure that is

drawn from income tax data. While there are some differences in coverage for these two
measures, any significant divergence between the two is most likely to reflect sampling error
in the HLFS.
• Meanwhile, the employer-focused Quarterly Employment Survey (QES) recorded a 0.1% rise
in the number of filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. (This survey is of course vulnerable
to sampling error as well.)
• We’re most inclined towards the MEI measure of employment, given the breadth of its

coverage. But our best interpretation is that, to the extent that jobs are growing, it’s not
yet fast enough to absorb the growth in the adult population – hence the further rise in the
unemployment rate.

### Page 4

Westpac New Zealand Economics
4
Labour force participation
• The surprising fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a drop in the participation rate
from 70.6% to 70.4%.
• These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were suggestive of sampling error in the
survey. Any such errors largely wash out when it comes to the unemployment rate, however,
which is why we recommend focusing on this measure.

• Setting aside the usual quarterly volatility, labour force participation remains caught
between two competing forces. One is cyclical weakness, especially among younger people,
who are finding it difficult to break into a “low-fire, low-hire” labour market. The teen
unemployment rate of around 25% is a record high going back to the start of the survey
in 1986.
• The other force is rising participation among the over-60s (who also make up a growing

share of the population). Kiwis have had a growing propensity to work beyond the NZ Super
age, as there is no penalty for doing so. It’s unclear to what extent this can be considered
an increase in the pool of available workers: older workers tend to have low mobility
between jobs, and if they find themselves out of work are more likely to leave the labour
force altogether.

### Page 5

Westpac New Zealand Economics
5
Unemployment and underutilisation
• The unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7% in the September quarter. This was the
highest rate since September 2015.
• While there are other indicators of spare capacity in the labour market, we’re not convinced
that there is additional information to draw from them this time.
• The broader underutilisation measure, which includes discouraged jobseekers and part-
time workers wanting more hours, was steady at 13.8% after having risen sharply from

12.9% last quarter. This measure is both higher (by definition) and more variable than the
unemployment rate, but tells a similar story in that it’s around an 11-year high.
• There was a marked 1.6% fall in the number of hours worked (although ‘usual’ hours were
down just 0.4%). However, we suspect this was largely driven by Matariki – a floating holiday
that won’t be captured in the seasonal adjustment.
• This may also account for the 2.5% rise in part-time employment, against a 0.7% fall in

full-time employment. Some workers may have been pushed into the part-time category
(less than 30 hours a week) simply by virtue of only working four days during the
survey week.

### Page 6

Westpac New Zealand Economics
6
Hiring
• Business confidence surveys have shown a lift in hiring intentions in recent months, as firms
have shaken off the initial shock of the Iran conflict and the surge in fuel prices.
• However, those stated intentions aren’t obviously turning into reality yet. Job advertisements
were down about 1% for the September quarter in seasonally adjusted terms, and they
remain well below pre-Covid levels. Job ads will capture ‘churn’ within existing roles as well

as new job creation, but both of these would be associated with a strong labour market.
• With the working-age population growing at the same time, the result was a modest fall in
the vacancy rate, one of the RBNZ’s highest-ranked measures of labour market tightness.
• We estimate that there are around 1.9 people unemployed for every job vacancy, up from
around 1.8 people this time last year.
• Firms have told us that they were reluctant to lay off workers during the economic downturn

in 2023-24, as they were wary of the extreme labour shortages that had developed in the
previous boom years. As a result, they may find themselves still overstaffed even as demand
has started to pick up again, and haven’t needed to return to hiring yet.

### Page 7

Westpac New Zealand Economics
7
Population and migration
• The working-age population grew by around 1.0% over the last year, compared to a peak of
3.3% growth over 2023. Jobs growth (based on the less volatile MEI measure) is still lagging
behind population growth on an annual basis.
• The pool of potential workers is generally becoming older, with the Baby Boomers
increasingly reaching pension age and beyond, and with migrant arrivals tending to be an

older cohort than we saw pre-Covid – more in their 30s and 40s than their 20s.
• Net migration flows have started to pick up from their lows since late 2025. Departures of
New Zealanders have come down from their highs, though they remain above pre-Covid
levels, as the relative appeal of the Australian jobs market has narrowed.
• Foreign arrivals have picked up to some degree, although the growth appears to have been
mostly driven by students rather than those on work visas.

### Page 8

Westpac New Zealand Economics
8
Sectoral and regional trends
• Jobs growth has been relatively strong in government-connected sectors such as healthcare
and education, as well as in local and central government administration.
• Mining and utilities have seen strong growth in percentage terms, but neither of these
sectors are big employers in absolute terms.
• The construction sector is now starting to see a lift in employment, after a steep decline

over 2024-25. Residential building consents have risen strongly over the last year or so,
pointing to a growing pipeline of work for the homebuilding industry.
• Sectors that are connected to international tourism, such as transport and recreational
services, had previously seen strong employment growth, but this appears to have flattened
out in recent months.
• Employment trends have generally been more positive in the South Island than the North,

which likely reflects the greater influence of the agricultural sector – while not a large
employer in itself, farmers have benefited from high export prices and their spending has
flowed into other sectors.

### Page 9

Westpac New Zealand Economics
9
Wages
• The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter. Annual growth
remained around the lowest since 2021.
• While the overall result was in line with our forecast, the details were a little weaker than
we expected, with less contribution from private sector wages (up 0.5%) and a surprisingly
large 0.7% increase in the public sector.
• The distribution of pay rates has returned to pre-Covid trends. Only 44% of roles saw a

pay rise over the last year; of those roles that did see an increase, the average size has
converged on the 2-3% range.
• The LCI is not a measure of what workers actually receive – it reflects the cost to businesses
for a given unit of labour – and hence shouldn’t be compared to the inflation rate. A more
suitable measure is the analytical unadjusted LCI, which includes pay rises that are related
to productivity gains. This measure slowed from 3.1% to 2.9% on an annual basis, the lowest

since March 2021.
• There was a marked pickup in the proportion of firms reporting cost-of-living adjustments as
the main reason for pay increases. However, the size of those increases varied greatly, with
many of them below the rate of CPI inflation.
• This seems to reflect a shift in the justification for pay rises, rather than an overall increase in
wage pressures – firms are more likely to cite cost-of-living adjustments because they’re less
likely to cite demand-side pressures.

### Page 10

Westpac New Zealand Economics
10
International comparisons
• New Zealand’s unemployment rate is the 18th lowest in the OECD (out of 38 countries). This
ranking has been broadly unchanged over the last year or so.
• Notably, New Zealand’s unemployment rate is substantially above Australia’s, whereas
historically it has been lower on average. This shift in relative job opportunities has been a
key attractor for New Zealanders over the last couple of years – as it was in the early 2010s

when the Australian economy was benefiting from a boom in mining construction.
• However, that gap between unemployment rates is no longer widening, with the Australian
jobs market starting to soften this year as the RBA has raised interest rates to rein in inflation
(which was already running too high before the Iran conflict and the surge in oil prices). We
expect Australia’s unemployment to rise further over the year ahead.
• New Zealand has the 7th highest employment rate in the OECD (down from 6th last quarter).

We tend to have high rates of labour force participation for 15-64 year olds, and particularly
high participation for the over-65s, as the lack of means testing for NZ Superannuation
means there is no financial disincentive to working beyond pension age.

Document 493: 【國泰證期研究部】個股速報-國巨(2327 TT)-營運槓桿顯現，獲利動能延續-20261029

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】個股速報-國巨(2327 TT)-營運槓桿顯現，獲利動能延續-20261029.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

國巨 2327 TT  ( 電子–零組件 ) 
營運槓桿顯現，獲利動能延續 
投資與評價 
1. 國巨 3Q26受惠稼動率提升及前次漲價效益反映，預估毛利率 41.1%、營益
率 29.7%，獲利為近八年新高；4Q26預期隨外溢訂單挹注及最新一輪漲價
逐步反映，營收及營業利益將再創歷史新高。 
2. 國巨 26年受惠 AI需求帶動出貨，交期拉長帶動漲價。預期 26年底村田可
能釋出非蘋 MLCC產能，27年隨日系供應商將產能轉向高階 AI MLCC，供
需缺口進一步擴大，預估 26年 EPS 21.21元、27年 EPS 45.70元。 
3. 目前國巨股價為 547元，約為 12X FY27E EPS，位於近三年 11～50倍 PER
參考區間下緣位置。三星電機 3Q26財報正向進展，更多 LTA簽訂並持續與
客戶協商中，且村田持續傳出 EOL消息。展望後市，預期國巨 AI產品占比
將隨整體 AI需求提升，中階產品外溢訂單持續成長，鉭質電容受惠高市占率
及定價能力，惟市場未能適當反應外溢訂單效應與 27 年漲價幅度，故維持
買進投資評等，微調 27年獲利預估，目標價上修至710元(15x FY27E PER)。 
重點更新 
營運槓桿顯現，預期國巨 3Q26獲利為近八年新高 
國巨 3Q26 受惠前次漲價效益開始反映，加上中階外溢訂單挹注帶動稼動率提
升，3Q26 營收為 495.31 億元(+11.4% QoQ)，與預估之 495.57億元(+11.5% 
QoQ)相近，並優於公司財測之溫和成長幅度。在產品組合改善下，調高毛利率預
估至 41.1%(+2.6 ppts QoQ)，營業費用率則降至 11.4%(-0.7 ppts QoQ)，營運
槓桿顯現，預估營業淨利季增 25.1%至 146.95 億元，營益率 29.7%。稅後淨利
預估為 119.45 億元(+26.8% QoQ)，EPS 5.80 元，毛利率連續第五季上揚，獲
利為近八年新高。

預期國巨 4Q26受惠多重成長動能延續，營收再創新高 
4Q26為客戶庫存檢視節點，客戶後續將增加下單，使供需更趨緊俏，進一步支
撐漲價。然而，以中低階 MLCC為主的消費性需求仍低迷，預期最新一輪漲價貢
獻遞延至 4Q26末。預估 4Q26營收為 534.61億元(+7.9% QoQ)，在稼動率持
續提升及產品組合優化下，上修毛利率預期至 44.2%(+3.1 ppts QoQ)。預期營
業淨利為 175.20億元(+19.2% QoQ)，稅後淨利將季增 19.7%達 142.95億元，
EPS 6.94元，受惠多重成長動能，營收及營業利益將再創歷史新高。

Document 526: 【金融產業攻略】美金融獲利成長貢獻轉向利收

Source PDF: all report pdf/【金融產業攻略】美金融獲利成長貢獻轉向利收.pdf

### Page 1

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
美金融獲利成長貢獻轉向利收
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
• 銀行獲利穩增長，利收動能接棒領
航，資本市場短暫休整 pg1
• 摩根大通：企業投資與消費穩健，
高利率紅利延續 pg2
• 花旗：上調2026利收預期、下調
壞帳率、對潛在風險謹慎 pg2
• 高盛：AI投資延續支撐成長，資本
市場韌性猶存 pg2
• 美國銀行：升息環境受惠，利收動
能可望再加速 pg3
重點摘要
本月關鍵圖表
• 大型銀行3Q26獲利成長延續，
但幅度收斂，主要是非利息收
入表現開始降溫，3Q獲利貢獻
焦點一致轉向了淨利息收入。
• 展望後市，因淨利息收入的前
景相對清晰，故可見美大型銀
行因此維持或上調2026全年淨
利息收入預期，或上修2026年
股東權益報酬率，並重申或下

修壞帳率。相比之下，資本市
場業務則需觀望，除了活躍度
已下滑外，交易收入在4Q時也
易出現季節性回落壓力。
• 管理層對經濟具韌性有相同的
共識，表示企業資本支出意願
與消費信貸需求並未因高利率
環境而明顯轉弱，2027年淨利
息收入仍具進一步增長空間。
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
金融 正向 利收接棒成長動能，升息紅利續撐獲利展望，管理層對經濟具韌性有相同共識
1
《銀行獲利穩增長，利收動能接棒領航，資本市場
短暫休整》
大型銀行 3Q26獲利成長延續 ，但幅度收斂 ，主要
是非利息收入表現開始降溫，多數銀行認為投行案
源充足，需求只是遞延而非消失，債權相關業績因
利率走高而下滑，但股權相關業績仍有收獲；相比
之下，3Q獲利貢獻焦點一致轉向了淨利息收入 ，
業績增長得益於貸款與存款穩定成長，而財富管理
業務也提供了顯著的成長。部份銀行非利息費用成
長幅度大於收入成長幅度，導致效率比率變差。儘
管信貸品質仍健康無虞，但仍有部份銀行對潛在風
險展現謹慎態度，因而提高壞帳準備金。

展望後市，因淨利息收入的前景相對清晰，故可見
美大型銀行因此維持或上調 2026全年淨利息收入
預期，或上修 2026年股東權益報酬率 ，並重申或
下修壞帳率。相比之下，資本市場業務則需觀望，

重點摘要
2
除了活躍度已下滑外，交易收入在4Q時也易出現季
節性回落壓力 。但各家管理層對經濟具韌性有相同
的共識，表示企業資本支出意願與消費信貸需求並
未因高利率環境而明顯轉弱 ，即便假設聯準會2027
年上半年之前升息 3次，2027年淨利息收入仍具進
一步增長空間。
《摩根大通：企業投資與消費穩健 ，高利率紅利延
續》
3Q26營收年增8%、每股盈餘年增 10%。淨利息收
入年增13%，受存款、信用卡及企業貸款成長帶動；
非利息收入年增3%；市場收入年增15%，由股票交
易大增帶動 ，固收業務下滑；投行業務年增 15%，
主要受到諮詢費收入成長所帶動；非利息費用支出
年增12%，效率比率變差 ，呆帳與準備金皆較去年

同期減少。公司上調2026年淨利息收入預估，並預
期2027年進一步成長，反映資產負債表擴張及高利
率環境利多 。管理層認為美國消費與企業投資仍具
韌性，惟通膨、升息、財政赤字及地緣政治風險仍
是後續觀察重點。
《花旗：上調2026利收預期、下調壞帳率、對潛在
風險謹慎》
3Q26營收年增8%、每股盈餘年增 53%。淨利息收
入成長14%，受惠服務、財富管理 、市場及信用卡
業務擴張；非利息收入下滑4%；市場收入受益於股
票收入成長；投資銀行收入受益於股權資本市場 ，
部份被併購諮詢與債權資本市場下滑所抵消；公司
增加壞帳準備金反映了管理潛在經濟風險的謹慎態
度；公司預期第四季市場收入將出現典型的季節性
下滑。公司上調2026年淨利息收入與股東權益報酬
率預期，下修壞帳率 。管理層看好美國消費與企業
活動韌性 ，認為高利率環境及 AI投資需求可持續支
撐成長。
《高盛：AI投資延續支撐成長 ，資本市場韌性猶存 》

Document 533: イランの緊張長期化と日米欧の金融政策 ～インフレ再燃で問われる金融引き締めの行方～

Source PDF: all report pdf/イランの緊張長期化と日米欧の金融政策 ～インフレ再燃で問われる金融引き締めの行方～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 43
Status: ok

### Page 1

イランの緊張長期化と日米欧の金融政策
～インフレ再燃で問われる金融引き締めの行方～
2026年11月４日
新家 義貴（日本）
桂畑 誠治（米国）
田中 理（欧州）

### Page 2

サマリー
1
⚫ イラン情勢の長期化で原油・天然ガス価格が高止まり。原油は日米欧で概ね連動する
一方、天然ガスは市場構造によって地域差が大きい。ホルムズ海峡の正常化後は原油
価格の緩やかな低下を想定するが、供給制約による上振れリスクが残る。
⚫ 資源高によるインフレ圧力が日米欧で再燃。ただし、川上から消費者物価への波及速
度は異なる。米国は比較的速やかに価格転嫁が進み、日本は遅れて長期化、ユーロ圏
はその中間に位置する。
⚫ 日本ではコスト高の価格転嫁が食料品・日用品などに波及し、2027年初の物価再加速
が見込まれる。日銀は基調物価の「２％到達」から「２％での定着」へ重点を移し、
12月以降も段階的な利上げを継続する見通し。
⚫ 米国では堅調な内需と雇用を背景に総合・コア物価とも上振れ。ＦＲＢは９月に利上
げを再開し、10月は据え置いたが、12月に追加利上げを予想。2027年も高い政策金
利水準が続く見込み。
⚫ ユーロ圏では資源高による物価上昇が続く一方、二次的な物価波及は今のところ限定

的。ＥＣＢは12月の追加利上げを見込むが、フランスの財政不安や長期金利上昇によ
る金融環境の引き締まりで、2027年の利上げ継続には不透明感が浮上している。

### Page 3

日米欧ともに緩やかな景気拡大が継続も、資源高によるインフレ圧力が高まる。
日銀は利上げ継続、ＦＲＢは利上げ再開、ＥＣＢは来年の利上げ継続に不透明感。

日本の物価・金融政策動向

### Page 11

日本：景気は緩やかな回復基調を維持
10
 日銀短観９月調査では、企業の業況判断は総じて良好な水準を維持。中東情勢の悪化に伴うコスト増加が重荷
となる一方、AI関連需要の拡大や価格転嫁の進展などが企業活動を下支え。
 設備投資計画も高い伸びを維持。人手不足に対応した省力化・効率化投資に加え、デジタル化・ AI関連投資など、
企業の設備投資意欲は引き続き旺盛。
 先行きも企業部門の底堅さが下支えとなり、景気は緩やかな回復基調を維持すると予想。ただし、資源高による物
価上振れで家計の節約志向が強まる可能性には注意が必要。

### Page 12

日本：根強い物価上昇圧力
11
 26年9月のCPIコアは前年比+2.1％と、25年12月以来の+２％台に。政府の各種支援による押し下げも大き
く、特殊要因を除くCPIは前年比+3.2％まで上昇。
 国内企業物価指数は高い伸びが続く。既往のコスト高の価格転嫁はなお進んでおり、プラスチック製品など川下に
相当する価格が急上昇。

### Page 14

日本：政府の物価高対策
13
 政府は、現在170円/ℓ程度に設定されているガソリン価格抑制の基準について、 12月以降、2027年春先に向け
て毎月５円程度ずつ引き上げる方針を表明。仮に20円/ℓ引き上げられた場合、CPIコアは0.2％Pt超押し上げ。
 一方、政府は26年12月～27年２月に電気・ガス料金補助を再開する方針も決定。補助がない場合と比べて、
ＣＰＩコアは瞬間風速で▲0.6％Pt程度押し下げられると試算されるが、前年も概ね同規模の支援が実施されて
いたため、前年比への影響は限られる。
 物価上昇圧力は強く、政府支援見直しを考慮しても、27年初のCPIコアは前年比+3％台に上昇する見込み。

日本：企業の物価見通しと2027年春闘の行方
14
 企業の中長期的な物価見通しは上昇傾向。企業が物価上昇を、一時的なコスト増ではなく長期的なものとして織
り込み始めている可能性。基調的な物価上昇率の上振れを警戒する必要。
 27年春闘に向けての動きがスタート。連合による賃上げの目安は前年対比でやや強め。経団連も経労委報告原案
で賃上げの「社会全体への浸透」を掲げる。労使ともにトーンダウンはみられない。 27年春闘の動向は、賃金・物価
の好循環が持続し、基調的な物価上昇率が２％程度で定着するかを判断するうえで重要な材料。
